# Gemma 4 Developer Agent: roadmap, master checklist, and milestones

Research date: 2026-09-30. Greenfield plan for one team, one Kaggle account, three Windows 11 machines. The companion execution schedule is [02-six-sprint-master-plan.md](02-six-sprint-master-plan.md).

This document is a specification for the submitted agent brain. It is based on the competition overview, data page, getting-started notebook, official rules, `HARNESS_README.md`, and the published training files in `gemma-4-developer-agent.zip` (129 tasks, the sample submission, and the sandbox Dockerfile). The live leaderboard page did not return numeric scores without an authenticated session. The figures 0.17, 0.15, and 0.15 are the public top three given for this date, and every score gate below uses them as the day-0 bar.

## 1. What a win actually is

The scored artifact is a single `submission.zip`. Kaggle unpacks it, compiles the YAML into a Google ADK agent, and runs that agent against a hidden test set of about 120 tasks drawn from private repositories. The public leaderboard is the public half of that set. The private leaderboard is the other half, and it decides prizes. Each team may upload one submission per day and, before the final deadline, designate two of them for judging.

A task scores 1 when the agent's unified diff applies on a fresh snapshot and `pytest` exits 0 with a valid JUnit report: at least one passing test, zero failures, zero errors, and every required fail-to-pass and pass-to-pass node present and not skipped. The competition score is the fraction of tasks that clear that bar. There is no partial credit.

| Item | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Event | Google - The Gemma 4 Developer Agent Competition |
| Sponsor | Google DeepMind / Google LLC |
| Start | 2026-09-23 |
| Paper track deadline | 2026-11-12, 23:59 UTC |
| Entry and team-merger deadline | 2026-11-25, 23:59 UTC |
| Final submission deadline | 2026-12-02, 23:59 UTC |
| Prediction prizes | $37,000 / $18,000 / $10,000 |
| Paper prizes | $35,000 pool, separate competition |
| Team size | 5 maximum |
| Uploads | 1 per day, 2 finals selected for judging |
| Metric | Resolution rate in \[0, 1\] |
| Day-0 public top three | 0.17, 0.15, 0.15 |
| Winner license | OSI license that allows commercial use; Apache-2.0 is the stated type |

Ties go to the earlier submission. That is a reason to upload a genuine contender as soon as it is reproducible, and a reason to avoid burning the clock on identical reruns.

## 2. What 0.17 means on day 0

The overview snapshot from 2026-09-29 shows 5,800 entrants, 864 participants, 829 teams, and 2,004 submissions. One upload per day means the field has had roughly a week of real attempts. The published sample is not a serious agent yet:

| Sample knob | Sample value | Harness default | Effect on the official notebook run |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `max_time_minutes` | 1 | 60 | Both demo tasks hit the wall at 60 seconds |
| `max_tool_calls` | 10 | 100 in `inference.py` | The demo used 4 calls and still died on time |
| `timeout_seconds` | 60 | 300 | A single slow command can consume the whole task |
| `thinking_budget` | 4096 | 4096 | Long thoughts delay the first edit |
| `max_output_tokens` | 16384 | 16384 | Large tool calls are cut off before the closing tool-call tag |
| Adapters | rank 4, layer 0 only, `q_proj` and `o_proj` | up to 8 adapters, rank ≤ 128 | Format samples, not trained policies |
| Outcome in the notebook | 0 patch characters | fallback diff is kept if the tree is dirty | Both tasks ended with an empty working tree |

About 120 hidden tasks share one 12-hour window, including sandbox setup and excluding Phase-2 grading. A 60-minute per-task cap would finish on the order of ten tasks and then leave the rest unscored. An empty diff on a timeout scores 0. The current public leaders are consistent with a small number of completed fixes on the public half (about 60 tasks): 9/60 = 0.15 and 10/60 ≈ 0.167, which displays as 0.17 at two decimals. One extra public solve is worth about two points in the second decimal. The standard error at p = 0.20 and n = 60 is about 0.05, so a 0.02 public move is noise. Local counts decide whether an upload is justified.

The hidden set was filtered so a larger frontier model can pass, or can come within a single test of passing. The tasks are solvable. The 31B quantized agent, the 32,768-token serving cap, and the wall clock are the constraints. A brain that reliably localizes, edits early, and submits will move the public score before any LoRA exists. Weight updates come after that scaffold is frozen.

## 3. The object being built

The brain is the contents of `submission.zip`. The compiler accepts declarative YAML, Markdown, skill scripts, and PEFT LoRA weights. It does not accept an `agent.py` entrypoint. Arbitrary Python runs only as a skill script inside the sandbox.

```text
submission.zip
├── agent.yaml
├── eval_config.yaml
├── configs/
│   └── sampling.yaml
├── prompts/
│   ├── coder.md
│   └── localizer.md
├── sub_agents/
│   └── localizer.yaml
├── skills/
│   ├── harvest_symbols/
│   ├── py_grep/
│   ├── pytest_node/
│   └── patch_hygiene/
└── adapters/                  # omitted until a real adapter beats the base model
    ├── localizer/
    └── editor/
```

Every agent declares `gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct`. That string is the only model the scorer accepts. Other Gemma 4 aliases exist for local CLI experiments and are illegal in a competition submission.

Tools the YAML may name:

| Tool | Counts as a tool call | Role in this brain |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `search_similar_code` | yes | Symbol-key retrieval over the precomputed graph embeddings |
| `get_code_neighbors` | yes | Callers, callees, and definitions around a resolved symbol |
| `get_code_subgraph` | yes | Induced subgraph once a handful of symbols are known |
| `read_file` | yes | Line-sliced view, hard-capped at 150 lines and 10,000 characters |
| `edit_file` | yes | 3-tier replace: exact, then whitespace-flexible, then delimiter-regex |
| `write_file` | yes | New files, and rewrites when replace cannot lock onto a span |
| `run_command` | yes | `/bin/bash -c` in `/workspace`, 5,000-character output cap |
| `get_status` | no | Free budget probe |
| `submit_patch` | no | Free. Ends the session once the turn finishes. Must be last |

`search_similar_code` does not embed a natural-language sentence. The sandbox is offline, so the tool resolves the query against node keys and suffixes in the `.npz` archive and then ranks neighbors by cosine similarity. The getting-started log shows the practical consequence: the query `Server Sent Events` returned zero nodes, and the query `SSE` returned an unrelated class. The sample system prompt tells the model to pass "keywords from the error message" to this tool. That instruction fights the implementation. The brain passes symbol-shaped strings only: a class, a function, or a dotted name harvested from the issue text.

Skill scripts share the sandbox filesystem with `run_command` and debit the same clock. They see `/workspace` and the preinstalled Python 3.13 environment. They do not see the NetworkX graph. Graph access stays on the three host tools. Skills are stdlib-only, because the image has no network and no PyPI.

## 4. Train-set facts that set the shape of the brain

Measured from `tasks.jsonl` in the local data package (129 lines, snapshot of the public training release):

| Fact | Measurement | Design consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Repositories | fastapi 67, rich 48, requests 13, httpx 1 | A FastAPI-specific prompt will look strong and transfer poorly |
| Issue dates | 2023-07-29 through 2026-06-20 | The set includes recent code, not a frozen historical dump |
| Hints | 0 / 129 non-empty | The `{hints}` slot stays in the prompt and is usually empty |
| Problem length | min 42, median 418, p90 1,891, max 10,096 characters; 38 under 200 | Localization from a short report is the main problem |
| Path or traceback in the issue | 15 mention a `.py` path; 13 look like a traceback | The agent must recover symbols that the issue never names as paths |
| Gold patch width | median 1 file, mean 2.16, max 26; 38 patches touch 2+ files | Fast path is one file; second path edits two or three files |
| Gold patch size | median about 12 changed lines, p90 128, max 12,714 | Optimize for a small hunk; accept the giant outlier as a miss |
| Where the gold edit lands | 0 gold patches touch test files; 34 file touches under `docs_src/` | Edit library code and executable examples; leave tests alone |
| Hidden tests | 121 / 129 test patches contain `assert` or `pytest.raises`; all test-file touches are under `tests/` | Exact error strings, status codes, and signatures decide pass/fail |
| Quoted material in the issue | 80 / 129 contain quotes or backticks | When the issue states a literal, the patch must reproduce it |

The Phase-2 harness deletes agent edits to test modules, `conftest.py`, `pytest.ini`, `pyproject.toml`, `tox.ini`, `setup.cfg`, and related runner files before it applies `test_patch`. A fix that lives only in a test file scores 0. Scratch scripts left under `/workspace` become part of `git diff HEAD`. Reproduction scripts belong in `/tmp`.

The agent never receives `test_patch`. Existing tests at `base_commit` are the pass-to-pass suite. The fail-to-pass tests arrive only during grading. "Run the test that shows the bug" is possible only when that test already exists. Otherwise the agent checks behavior with a `/tmp` probe and existing tests around the edited file, then submits.

## 5. Binding constraints

### Serving

| Knob | Competition value |
| --- | --- |
| GPUs | 4 × NVIDIA L4, 24 GB each, tensor parallel 4 |
| Weights | `gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct`, W4A16 compressed-tensors, about 16–18 GB |
| Context | `max_model_len` 32,768 tokens, including the prompt, thoughts, and tool calls |
| Activations | bfloat16 |
| LoRA slots | `max_loras` 8, `max_lora_rank` 128 |
| Tool parser | `gemma4`, with thinking enabled on the server |
| Unpacked zip | under 3 GiB, including adapters |
| Per-container sandbox | 4 GiB RAM, 2 vCPU, `network_mode=none`, image `python:3.13-slim` plus git, patch, pytest |

Gemma 4 31B's native window is far above 32k (public model cards describe 128k–256k, with a large share of sliding-window layers). The scorer will not serve that window. Prompts, skills, and adapters are designed for 32,768 tokens. Context compaction is also fixed by `scripts/inference.py`: every 5 events after a 14,336-token threshold, overlap 2, retain 5. Early localization has to be short enough to survive compaction, or it has to be re-read from a skill resource.

Rank-32 LoRA on the full set of linear projections is on the order of a few hundred megabytes per adapter. Two real adapters fit easily. Rank 128 is available and is held back until a rank-32 adapter has a measured gain, because the 3 GiB cap and the 5090 training budget both prefer the smaller file.

### Wall clock

Conservative planning assumption: one scoring job processes the full hidden set of about 120 tasks inside 12 hours. Wheel install and model load take the first slice of that window. The getting-started notebook spent real time on 41 wheels before it evaluated anything.

```text
agent_minutes ≈ (720 − startup_minutes − task_count × setup_minutes) / task_count
```

| Tasks in the job | Startup (min) | Setup each (min) | Mean minutes left for the agent | Per-task cap to set |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 60 | 20 | 1.0 | 10.7 | 10 |
| 60 | 20 | 2.0 | 9.7 | 9 |
| 120 | 20 | 1.0 | 4.8 | 5 |
| 120 | 20 | 2.0 | 3.8 | 4 |

The first Kaggle log retires this table. If the run states that it iterated about 60 tasks, the cap moves to the top rows. If it iterates about 120, the cap stays near 5 minutes. Until that log exists, the lab and the first uploads use a 6-minute cap and a 24-call cap so a stuck task dies with a dirty tree instead of reading until the clock is gone.

A second clock mismatch is hardware. Local decode on an RTX 5090 will not match 4× L4 with tensor parallel. Sprint 1 records Kaggle seconds per model turn and local seconds per model turn, and scales `max_time_minutes` by that ratio before any later upload.

### Rules that affect the lab

- External data is allowed when it is public and free, or cheap enough to meet the host's reasonableness standard. A small Gemini subscription is the host's own example of an acceptable cost. A paid proprietary bug dataset priced like the prize pool is not.
- Code used to produce the winning submission is released under a commercial-use OSI license. Training code, eval scripts, and the data manifest have to stay reproducible for the winner write-up.
- Private sharing of competition code is disqualifying. The three machines belong to one participant and one Kaggle account. They are a lab, not a second team.
- Hand-labeling of test records is forbidden. The hidden set is never inspected manually because its issues are not released. Train-set gold patches may be studied locally.
- One account, one team, maximum five people. This plan is written for a single operator.

## 6. Design space

Four shapes were considered against the constraints above.

**Long-horizon repository agent.** A free tool loop in the style of a cloud coding agent, with dozens of reads and a late edit. This matches the harness's tool list and the sample prompt's "explore then fix" story. It loses on the clock. The official two-task demo spent its minute in `find` and `grep` and finished with an empty diff. A timeout fallback can only submit what is already on disk.

**One-shot retrieval and patch.** Retrieve a file, emit a unified diff, submit. This matches the median train patch of one file and about a dozen lines. It has no legal second turn when `edit_file` misses, and 38 of 129 gold patches touch more than one file. It is the right inner step, and a weak outer loop.

**Train first.** QLoRA or a reinforcement-learning run on the 31B QAT checkpoint before the scaffold is stable. Reward is a single bit per multi-minute episode. The published adapters touch layer 0 only, so there is no trained policy to refine. Unparsable tool calls dominate the gradient until the prompt, the parser, and the budgets are frozen.

**Repository specialists.** Separate prompts that know `fastapi/routing.py`, `rich/console.py`, and `requests/utils.py`. Those files dominate the train gold patches. The test repositories are private and were built with the same pipeline, so a hotspot prior from FastAPI is a leaky shortcut. The transfer check is the 13 `requests` tasks plus the single `httpx` task, held out of every prompt example and every training mixture.

**Chosen brain: budgeted localize-then-edit.** One root coder with a low thinking budget. One read-only localizer exposed as an `AgentTool` with `skip_summarization: true`. Four stdlib skills for harvesting symbols, searching Python, running one test node, and checking the diff. The first edit lands as soon as the localizer names a file, so a timeout still yields a patch. `submit_patch` is an explicit final action after a hygiene script. LoRA starts in sprint 4 on traces from this frozen scaffold. A second round of rejection sampling lands in sprint 5. Reinforcement learning is a paper-track experiment and becomes a submission component only if it beats the rejection-sampling adapter on the frozen canary.

The localizer is a sub-agent because its `read_file` dumps must stay out of the coder's 32k window. The harness calls this out directly. The root agent stays a single `LlmAgent` in v1, because a `SequentialAgent` pipeline adds model turns under a five-minute cap. A sequential pipeline is the sprint-2 alternate if traces show the root wandering. It is not the default.

## 7. End-to-end roadmap

| Stage | Dates | Output | Advance when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0. Lab | 2026-09-30 | Three machines, official wheelhouse, Docker sandbox, model loaded at 32,768 context | One docker-backed task completes and writes a trace |
| 1. Ceiling and clock | 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-07 | Blind baseline, file-oracle ceiling, first full-coverage upload | Canary ≥ 3/20 and a Kaggle log that visits every task |
| 2. Localization | 2026-10-08 to 2026-10-14 | Symbol harvest, graph queries, early edit | Canary ≥ 6/20 and requests holdout ≥ 2/13 |
| 3. Repair and hygiene | 2026-10-15 to 2026-10-21 | One repair turn, patch contract, failure taxonomy | Canary ≥ 8/20; F7, F8, and F11 each under 10% of dev failures |
| 4. Editor and localizer LoRA | 2026-10-22 to 2026-10-28 | Rank-32 adapters that beat the base on canary | Canary gain ≥ 2 tasks over the frozen prompt-only brain |
| 5. Rejection sampling | 2026-10-29 to 2026-11-04 | Second-round adapters, budget retune from the speed ratio | OOD requests ≥ 4/13 and canary ≥ 11/20 |
| 6. Freeze and paper | 2026-11-05 to 2026-11-11 | Two tagged candidates, paper draft | Both candidates reproduce their canary score twice |
| Final window | 2026-11-12 to 2026-12-02 | Paper submitted, two finals selected | Private-side robustness preferred when public scores are within 0.05 |

Public-score ambitions, contingent on the ceiling study:

| Gate | Public target | Reading if the public half is ~60 tasks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| End of sprint 1 | ≥ 0.17 | Match the day-0 leader once the clock and the tool contract are fixed |
| End of sprint 2 | ≥ 0.25 | About 15 public fixes |
| End of sprint 3 | ≥ 0.32 | About 19 public fixes |
| End of sprint 4 | ≥ 0.38 | Adapter gain visible outside the train repos |
| End of sprint 5 | ≥ 0.42 | Rejection-sampling gain |
| Finals, 2026-12-01 | best stable score | Second final is the more conservative OOD candidate |

If the sprint-1 file-oracle ceiling on the canary is 5/20 or lower, later gates drop with it. The ceiling is an edit-and-specification limit: the agent is handed the gold file paths and still has to write a correct patch. A low ceiling shifts sprints 2 and 3 toward literal matching (error strings, signatures, status codes) and away from more graph tooling. A high ceiling with a low blind score shifts them toward localization. That single experiment is the fork in the roadmap.

## 8. Specification of the brain

### 8.1 Session policy

The root instruction is a contract, not a persona essay. It states the environment numbers the harness also injects, so the model sees them twice: 150-line reads, 5,000-character command output, no network, no `pip install`, tests and runner config are off limits, scratch lives in `/tmp`.

Ordered policy:

1. Call `harvest_symbols` on the issue text, or emit the same identifier list directly when the issue is already a list of names. Output is a JSON list of dotted symbols, exception class names, and quoted literals. Natural-language words are dropped.
2. Call the localizer `AgentTool` once with those symbols. The localizer may call `search_similar_code`, `get_code_neighbors`, `get_code_subgraph`, and `read_file`. It returns at most three files, each with a start line, an end line, and a one-sentence edit.
3. Read the named spans and apply the first `edit_file` before any further search. `old_string` is copied from the `read_file` body. One hunk per call. `allow_multiple` stays false.
4. If the localizer returned two or three files, edit those next. Stop at three files.
5. Run `pytest_node` on the existing test module closest to the edit, with a node id or `-k` expression when one is obvious. Then run one `/tmp` behavior probe when the issue states an observable.
6. On a failure that names the edited file, take one repair edit. On a collection error, a missing fixture, or a failure in an unrelated file, stop.
7. Run `patch_hygiene`. Delete anything it flags under `/workspace`. Call `submit_patch`. Reply with a short summary so the turn ends.

`get_status` is available and is not required on every turn. The root calls it when a tool response carries `budget_warning`.

### 8.2 Localizer contract

The localizer prompt tells it four mechanical facts:

- Queries to `search_similar_code` are exact symbol names, then suffixes, then case-insensitive variants. A sentence is an empty result.
- `get_code_neighbors` returns names. The node source is retrieved with a follow-up `search_similar_code` on that name or a sliced `read_file`.
- `read_file` ranges stay inside 150 lines. A second slice is allowed when `is_truncated` is true and the symbol is lower in the file.
- The reply is a fixed block the coder can apply after compaction: `symbols`, `files`, `literals`, `edit_plan`. No narrative.

The localizer has no `edit_file`, no `write_file`, no `run_command`, and no `submit_patch`. It cannot dirty the tree.

### 8.3 Skills

Each skill is a directory with `SKILL.md` frontmatter `name: <skill-name>`. Scripts use the standard library only. Exact tool argument names are copied from the installed `adk-submission` wheel on day 1, because the README names `run_skill_script` and `load_skill_resource` and the wheel is the authority for parameters.

| Skill | Script does | Why it is a script |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `harvest_symbols` | Regex for dotted names, `CamelCase`, `snake_case`, quoted literals, and exception tokens | Stops the model from sending English into `search_similar_code` |
| `py_grep` | `grep -n -R` limited to `*.py`, skipping `.git`, with a hard line cap | The demo `grep -r` hit packfiles and flooded the 5,000-character cap |
| `pytest_node` | `python3 -m pytest <node> -q --tb=line` with a 45-second timeout | Bare `pytest` fills the task budget on pre-existing failures |
| `patch_hygiene` | `git diff --stat` plus a denylist of test and config paths, and a list of untracked files outside `/tmp` | Catches scratch files and test edits before `submit_patch` |

`load_skill_resource` points at a one-page patch contract the coder can reload: copy `old_string` from the tool output, preserve indentation, reproduce quoted literals, leave tests untouched, put probes in `/tmp`.

### 8.4 Sampling

Milestone runs use temperature 0 and a fixed seed so a canary delta is attributable to the change under test. The submission uses the same settings. Thinking is enabled, with a budget swept in sprint 1 across 512, 1024, 2048, and 4096 tokens. The working hypothesis is that 1024 tokens wins under a five-minute cap, because a 4096-token thought can consume most of a turn on the grader. `thinking_level` is omitted. The harness maps it to an OpenAI reasoning-effort field that vLLM drops. `max_output_tokens` starts at 4096 so a tool call is small enough to close. Larger edits are split, which is also what the truncation nudge asks for.

### 8.5 Budgets in `eval_config.yaml`

Initial submission values, replaced after the first Kaggle log:

```yaml
evaluation:
  timeout_seconds: 90
  max_tool_calls: 24
  max_time_minutes: 6
  max_turns: 40
```

`submit_patch` and `get_status` do not consume the call budget. The harness still extracts a dirty tree when the agent dies without calling `submit_patch`. Early edit is what makes that fallback worth something. Explicit `submit_patch` remains the normal ending, because the session stops cleanly and the hygiene script has already run.

### 8.6 Adapters

No adapter is shipped until it beats the base model by at least two canary tasks at temperature 0. The sample rank-4 layer-0 adapters are schema examples and stay out of scored zips.

Training, when it starts:

| Adapter | Examples | Sequence length | Rank / alpha | Target modules |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `localizer` | Issue text to the structured file card, built from gold hunks and from synthetic bugs | 2k–4k | 32 / 64 | attention and MLP linears |
| `editor` | File slice plus issue literals to the `edit_file` arguments | 4k–8k | 32 / 64 | attention and MLP linears |

The chat template and the tool-call markers are those of the `gemma4` parser the scorer runs. Sprint 4 begins with a format-lock test: 20 hand-checked calls must parse under the same vLLM flags as the competition (`tool_call_parser=gemma4`, `reasoning_parser=gemma4`). An adapter that raises tool-call success and lowers canary resolution is discarded.

Data mixture for the editor, in order of trust:

1. Student trajectories on the LoRA pool that Phase-2 actually resolved. These match the tool schema by construction.
2. Synthetic bugs in permissively licensed public Python repos, with a local fail-to-pass test written before the patch. Same scaffold, same tools.
3. Teacher-drafted repairs, only for pool tasks the student missed, and only after the draft is rewritten into the nine-tool schema and re-checked by Phase 2.

The teacher is an external model under the reasonableness rule. It is a data source. It is never called at submission time. `requests` and `httpx` are absent from this mixture. So is the frozen canary.

### 8.7 Splits

Seed `20260930`. Counts:

| Split | Composition | Use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OOD holdout | all 13 `psf/requests` + the 1 `encode/httpx` | Transfer check. No prompt quotes, no training |
| Canary | 12 fastapi + 8 rich | Milestone scores only. Frozen on 2026-10-01 |
| Dev | 18 fastapi + 12 rich | Daily prompt and skill iteration |
| LoRA pool | 37 fastapi + 28 rich | Trajectory mining and adapter training |

Canary membership is written to `splits/canary.txt` on the first day of sprint 1 and then left alone. Reporting is in counts: `canary 8/20`, `requests 4/13`. Rates are secondary.

## 9. How the brain is tested and improved

### 9.1 Fidelity

A local number is comparable to Kaggle only when all of the following hold:

- The model directory is the competition QAT checkpoint.
- vLLM is launched with `max_model_len=32768`, the `gemma4` parsers, thinking enabled, and bfloat16 activations.
- The sandbox is the dataset `Dockerfile.sandbox` (`python:3.13-slim`, git, patch, pytest, the `imp` and `telnetlib` shims), with 4 GiB RAM, 2 CPUs, and no network.
- Phase 2 resets protected test and config paths before applying `test_patch`.
- The agent process cannot read `test_patch` or the gold `patch`. The grader can. An audit script greps the submission tree for any gold hunk before packaging.

The subprocess sandbox in the getting-started notebook failed `ensurepip`. It is used for parser smoke tests. Resolution numbers come from Docker.

### 9.2 Ceiling ladder

Run on the canary, temperature 0, before prompt iteration consumes the week:

| Rung | What the agent is given | What it measures |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gold patch replay | The official `patch`, applied by the harness | Confirms the lab's Phase 2 matches the host. Expect 20/20 |
| File oracle | Gold file paths in the user message, agent still edits | Edit and literal-matching ceiling |
| Symbol oracle | Gold symbol names, agent still searches and edits | Graph-tool ceiling |
| Blind | The issue text only | The real brain |

The gap from file oracle to blind is the localization backlog. The gap from gold replay to file oracle is the editing and specification backlog. Sprint 2 and sprint 3 staff the larger gap.

### 9.3 Failure taxonomy

Every unresolved dev or canary task gets one primary label from the trace and the Phase-2 log.

| Code | Pattern | First response |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | Timeout or nudge limit, empty tree | Force an earlier edit; cut thinking |
| F2 | Non-empty diff that fails all four apply passes | Smaller hunks; `edit_file` instead of a hand-built diff |
| F3 | Only protected paths changed, so the reset wipes the fix | Hygiene script; stronger prompt denylist |
| F4 | Right file, behavior still wrong | One repair turn fed by the pytest line |
| F5 | Behavior right, literal wrong (message, code, signature) | Copy literals from the issue into the localizer card |
| F6 | Fail-to-pass would pass and a pass-to-pass test broke | Narrower edit; rerun the neighboring existing test |
| F7 | `FileEditError` or a mismatched `old_string` | Re-read the span; paste from tool output |
| F8 | Truncated tool-call tag or a MAX_TOKENS nudge | Lower `max_output_tokens`; split the edit |
| F9 | `search_similar_code` count 0 on an English query | Symbol harvest in front of every graph call |
| F10 | Command timeout on a wide pytest | `pytest_node` only |
| F11 | Diff contains `/workspace` scratch or caches | Hygiene script; probes in `/tmp` |
| F12 | Edit assumes a FastAPI or Rich layout on another repo | Delete the repo-specific example from the prompt |

### 9.4 Improvement loop

One change per day on the dev split. A change graduates when dev improves by two or more tasks and the same change does not drop the OOD holdout. Canary is scored at milestone boundaries, not after every prompt tweak. Public uploads happen when the canary has moved by two or more tasks since the last upload, or when the upload is a scheduled measurement (clock calibration, speed ratio, final confirmation). The daily upload right is not a daily obligation.

Rejection sampling, sprint 5: for each LoRA-pool task, draw four student trajectories at temperature 0.3 during offline generation, keep those Phase 2 marks resolved, and supervise the next adapter on the kept traces plus the sprint-4 mixture. The submitted agent still runs at temperature 0, one trajectory, because the scorer does not implement best-of-n.

### 9.5 Paper track

The paper competition closes on 2026-11-12 and pays from a separate $35,000 pool. The write-up is the lab notebook of this brain: the clock model, the symbol-query graph contract, the ceiling ladder, and the adapter ablation. It cites the train set and local ablations. It does not depend on a new research project in the last week. Graph and embedding use is encouraged by the host and is already on the critical path, so the paper has a natural subject. The prediction entry remains the priority when the two calendars conflict on 2026-11-11.

## 10. Milestones

| ID | Date | Name | Exit evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M0 | 2026-09-30 | Lab up | `nvidia-smi` sees the 5090 inside WSL; sandbox image builds; one Docker eval writes `summary.json` |
| M1 | 2026-10-03 | Ceiling known | Gold replay 20/20 on canary; file-oracle and blind counts written down |
| M2 | 2026-10-07 | Clock known | First Kaggle log lists task coverage and turn latency; public score ≥ 0.17 or a written reason tied to the ceiling |
| M3 | 2026-10-14 | Localization works | F9 under 10% of dev failures; canary ≥ 6/20; requests ≥ 2/13 |
| M4 | 2026-10-21 | Prompt-only freeze | Canary ≥ 8/20; tagged `prompt-freeze` zip; failure histogram archived |
| M5 | 2026-10-28 | Adapters earn their place | Format-lock 20/20; canary ≥ prompt-freeze + 2 tasks |
| M6 | 2026-11-04 | Second-round brain | requests ≥ 4/13; canary ≥ 11/20; speed-ratio budget update uploaded if the ratio moved |
| M7 | 2026-11-11 | Candidates frozen | Two zips reproduce their canary score on two consecutive runs |
| M8 | 2026-11-12 | Paper in | Paper-track upload, or a written decision to skip |
| M9 | 2026-11-25 | Entry locked | Rules accepted, team roster final, no merger debt |
| MF | 2026-12-01 | Finals selected | Two submissions explicitly selected; 2026-12-02 left unused unless a selected zip is broken |

## 11. Master checklist

### Lab and rules

- [ ] Accept the competition rules under the single account that will submit (M0, before any data copy beyond the local package).
- [ ] Record the team roster. This plan assumes one operator. A merger later must respect the submission-count cap.
- [ ] Install the prediction stack on the PowerSpec from the day-0 procedure in the master plan (M0).
- [ ] Install the grading farm on the ROG desktop and the authoring environment on the ProArt laptop (M0).
- [ ] Pin the competition wheelhouse, the model revision, the sandbox image digest, and the vLLM launch flags in `env/LOCK.md` (M0).
- [ ] Confirm `max_model_len=32768` resident on the 5090 and record peak VRAM (M0).
- [ ] Confirm a Docker eval of one training task produces a trace, a patch file, and a Phase-2 log (M0).

### Measurement

- [ ] Write `splits/canary.txt`, `splits/dev.txt`, `splits/pool.txt`, and `splits/ood.txt` with seed `20260930` (M1).
- [ ] Run gold-patch replay on the canary and reach 20/20. A miss here is a harness bug, and prompt work waits (M1).
- [ ] Run file-oracle, symbol-oracle, and blind on the canary. File the counts (M1).
- [ ] Choose the sprint 2–3 emphasis from those counts: localization if the file oracle leads the blind run by 4 or more tasks; literal repair if the file oracle itself is at or below 5/20 (M1).
- [ ] Sweep thinking budgets 512, 1024, 2048, 4096 on ten dev tasks. Freeze the winner (M2).
- [ ] Upload the calibration zip. Archive the Kaggle log, the task count, and the seconds per turn (M2).
- [ ] Compute the speed ratio and rewrite `eval_config.yaml` if Kaggle turns are slower than local turns by 1.3× or more (M2).

### Scaffold

- [ ] Replace the sample system prompt. Remove keyword search against `search_similar_code` and the order to run a full-suite pytest (M2).
- [ ] Ship `harvest_symbols`, `py_grep`, `pytest_node`, and `patch_hygiene` after each script passes a fixture test inside the sandbox image (M3).
- [ ] Wire the read-only localizer as an `AgentTool` with `skip_summarization: true` (M3).
- [ ] Implement the early-edit rule and verify on a trace that the first `edit_file` happens before the fourth tool call on a median-length issue (M3).
- [ ] Add the single repair turn and the denylist for protected paths (M4).
- [ ] Label every unresolved dev task with F1–F12 and archive the histogram with the prompt-freeze tag (M4).
- [ ] Grep the submission tree for gold-hunk contamination before every upload (every upload).

### Weights

- [ ] Stand up QLoRA on the 5090 against the QAT checkpoint using the competition chat template (M5).
- [ ] Pass the 20-call format-lock test under the competition parser flags (M5).
- [ ] Train `localizer` and `editor` on the pool only. Compare each, and the pair, against the prompt-freeze brain on canary and on requests (M5).
- [ ] Discard any adapter that fails to gain two canary tasks (M5).
- [ ] Mine four trajectories per pool task, keep Phase-2 successes, train the second-round adapters (M6).
- [ ] Re-run the OOD holdout before and after the second round. A requests regression of two or more tasks rolls back the adapter (M6).

### Shipping

- [ ] Package with `agent.yaml` at the zip root. Confirm unpacked size, extension allowlist, one root config, and one declared model (every upload).
- [ ] Tag the git revision that produced each uploaded zip (every upload).
- [ ] Keep a submission log: date, tag, canary, requests, public score, and the one-sentence hypothesis (every upload).
- [ ] Freeze two candidates and reproduce each canary score twice (M7).
- [ ] Submit the paper, or record the skip, on 2026-11-12 (M8).
- [ ] Confirm rules acceptance and final roster before 2026-11-25 (M9).
- [ ] Select the two finals on 2026-12-01. Leave 2026-12-02 as a spare slot (MF).

## 12. Risks

| Risk | Why it is plausible | Response |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 32,768 tokens do not fit on the 5090 | Weights are ~17 GB; KV cache on top of a 32 GB card is the open measurement | Day-0 VRAM probe. If it OOMs, enable KV cache quantization and re-measure. Local scores at a shorter window are marked non-comparable |
| The scoring job contains ~120 tasks and setup is slow | Private repos may be larger than the train snapshots | First log rewrites the cap the same day. Early-edit keeps short tasks cheap so the cap is a maximum, not a target |
| 4× L4 is much slower per turn than the 5090 | Tensor parallel and a smaller memory bandwidth per GPU | Speed ratio from the calibration log scales the budget before sprint 2 |
| File-oracle ceiling is low | Hidden tests demand literals the issue never states | Shift sprint 3 to literal extraction. Lower the public target instead of adding more search |
| Adapters overfit FastAPI | 67 of 129 train tasks are one repo | Hold out requests and httpx. Require an OOD check before an adapter ships |
| Teacher traces use the wrong tool format | A frontier model writes prose patches | Keep a trace only after it parses and Phase 2 passes under the student scaffold |
| Public score chasing | n ≈ 60, one task ≈ 0.017, noise ≈ 0.05 | Upload on canary deltas of 2+ tasks, plus the scheduled measurements |
| Compaction drops the plan | Threshold is 14,336 tokens and retention is short | Localizer return is a compact card; the patch contract is a reloadable skill resource |
| Docker or driver drift | A newer vLLM changes `gemma4` tool parsing | Pin the wheelhouse. Format-lock test runs after every environment change |
| Power and disk on the AUX desktop | 550 W supply, 512 GB SSD, snapshots are 21 GB compressed and larger on disk | Snapshots live on the 2 TB HDD. The 3080 stays idle. The SSD holds the OS, the image, and the hot canary subset |
| Winner documentation is an afterthought | The license and the write-up are conditions of payment | Training commands and `env/LOCK.md` are kept current from day 0 |

## 13. Sources

- Competition overview, data page, getting-started notebook, and official rules, saved under `input/kagglecomp/docs/markdown/` (pages fetched 2026-09-29) and the live overview at `https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/gemma-4-developer-agent/overview`.
- `HARNESS_README.md` and the sample submission, `tasks.jsonl`, and `docker/Dockerfile.sandbox` inside `input/kagglecomp/kagglecomp_data_package/gemma-4-developer-agent.zip`.
- Getting-started notebook run log: both demo tasks timed out at 1 minute with an empty patch; `search_similar_code("Server Sent Events")` returned no nodes.
- Gemma 4 QAT deployment notes used for weight size and the single-GPU fit of `gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct`: Google's QAT write-up (2026-06-05) and the public compressed-tensors vLLM recipe. The competition harness, not those articles, is the authority for the 4× L4 serving flags.

# Master plan: day 0 through six sprints

Companion to [01-roadmap-checklist-milestones.md](01-roadmap-checklist-milestones.md). That document specifies the brain, the splits, the failure codes, and the milestones. This document is the schedule that builds it on the three machines below, starting from a Windows 11 bare-metal setup on 2026-09-30 and ending with two selected finals before 2026-12-02 23:59 UTC.

One person, one Kaggle account. The three PCs are a single lab. Nothing is submitted from a second account.

## 1. Hardware roles

| Machine | CPU and GPU | Memory and disk | Job in this lab |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PowerSpec G914, Windows 11 Pro | Ryzen 9 9950X3D, RTX 5090 32 GB | 64 GB DDR5-6000, 2 TB NVMe, 5 GbE | Official-fidelity inference, QLoRA, milestone evals, Kaggle packaging |
| ASUS ProArt P16 H7606, Windows 11 Home | Ryzen AI 9 HX 370, RTX 4060 8 GB | 32 GB LPDDR5X, 1 TB SSD | Prompt and trace review, paper, submission log. The 4060 does not run the 31B model |
| ASUS ROG STRIX G15CF, Windows 11 | i7-12700F, RTX 3080 10 GB, 550 W supply | 64 GB DDR, 512 GB SSD + 2 TB HDD | Phase-2 grading farm and snapshot archive. The 3080 stays idle |

The competition scorer is 4× L4, tensor parallel 4, `max_model_len=32768`. The 5090 is the only local GPU that can hold `gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct` (about 17 GB of W4A16 weights) with context headroom. The 4060 (8 GB) and the 3080 (10 GB) are below that weight footprint. They are used for everything around the model: editing, Docker sandboxes, and `pytest`.

Local decode speed will differ from 4× L4. Sprint 1 measures the ratio and rescales per-task budgets. A trajectory that fits in six minutes on the 5090 is not assumed to fit in six minutes on the grader until that measurement exists.

## 2. Calendar

| Block | Dates | Weekdays | Milestone |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Day 0 | 2026-09-30 | Wednesday | M0 lab up |
| Sprint 1 | 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-07 | Thursday to Wednesday | M1 ceiling, M2 clock |
| Sprint 2 | 2026-10-08 to 2026-10-14 | Thursday to Wednesday | M3 localization |
| Sprint 3 | 2026-10-15 to 2026-10-21 | Thursday to Wednesday | M4 prompt-only freeze |
| Sprint 4 | 2026-10-22 to 2026-10-28 | Thursday to Wednesday | M5 adapters |
| Sprint 5 | 2026-10-29 to 2026-11-04 | Thursday to Wednesday | M6 second-round brain |
| Sprint 6 | 2026-11-05 to 2026-11-11 | Thursday to Wednesday | M7 two candidates |
| Paper day | 2026-11-12 | Thursday | M8 |
| Entry deadline | 2026-11-25 | Wednesday | M9 |
| Select finals | 2026-12-01 | Tuesday | MF |
| Spare slot | 2026-12-02 | Wednesday | Use only if a selected zip is invalid |

All competition deadlines are 23:59 UTC. 23:59 UTC is 18:59 in US Central and 15:59 in US Pacific on these winter dates. The operator picks finals on Tuesday 2026-12-01 local evening so a timezone mistake cannot miss Wednesday's deadline.

## 3. Score gates

Gates are counts on frozen splits, plus a public score when an upload happened. The public top three on 2026-09-30 are 0.17, 0.15, and 0.15.

| When | Canary (of 20) | Requests holdout (of 13) | Public, if uploaded |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sprint 1 exit | ≥ 3 | measured, any | ≥ 0.17 |
| Sprint 2 exit | ≥ 6 | ≥ 2 | ≥ 0.25 |
| Sprint 3 exit | ≥ 8 | ≥ 3 | ≥ 0.32 |
| Sprint 4 exit | ≥ prompt-freeze + 2 | no drop of 2 or more | ≥ 0.38 |
| Sprint 5 exit | ≥ 11 | ≥ 4 | ≥ 0.42 |
| Sprint 6 exit | reproduced twice | reproduced once | best stable |

If the sprint-1 file oracle scores 5/20 or below, the public column is revised down that week and the revision is written into `lab/GATES.md`. A missed canary gate pauses the next sprint's new work until the failure histogram says why.

## 4. Repository layout on the PowerSpec

Created on day 0. The NVMe holds hot data. Paths assume the data disk is `D:`; if Windows and data share one volume, use `C:\gemma4` and keep 400 GB free.

```text
D:\gemma4\
├── data\                  # unpacked competition package
│   ├── tasks.jsonl
│   ├── snapshots\
│   ├── graphs\
│   ├── embeddings\
│   ├── wheels\
│   ├── docker\
│   └── sample_submission\
├── models\
│   └── gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct\
├── wheelhouse\            # competition Linux wheels, used inside WSL
├── repo\                  # this project: agent, skills, eval harness wrappers
│   ├── submission\
│   ├── splits\
│   ├── env\LOCK.md
│   ├── lab\GATES.md
│   └── results\
└── docker\                # sandbox build context copied from data\docker
```

WSL Ubuntu sees this tree at `/mnt/d/gemma4`. Model weights and Docker's data root live on the NVMe, not on a 9p mount, once day 0 has checked throughput. If `/mnt/d` reads slowly under vLLM, the weights are copied to `~/models` inside the ext4 VHDX and `LOCK.md` records which copy is authoritative.

## 5. Day 0 — Wednesday 2026-09-30

Goal: a reproducible Windows 11 lab that can serve the competition model and grade one task in Docker before midnight. No upload today.

### 5.1 PowerSpec firmware and Windows

Do this before installing WSL. A unstable EXPO profile or an old BIOS wastes the rest of the week on random CUDA faults.

1. Update the MSI MAG X870E Tomahawk BIOS to the current stable release for the Ryzen 9000 series, using MSI's own package. Load optimized defaults after the flash.
2. Enable SVM (AMD-V). Enable Above 4G Decoding and Resizable BAR. Set the boot drive to the 2 TB NVMe.
3. Enable EXPO for DDR5-6000. Boot a memory test (Windows Memory Diagnostic or MemTest86) for one pass. If it errors, clear EXPO and rerun. Training stability outranks the rated speed.
4. Leave PBO at the motherboard default. Record the BIOS version in `env/LOCK.md`.
5. Install Windows 11 Pro fresh if the machine is new or the current install is unknown. During setup, name the account, install pending Windows updates, and reboot until Windows Update is idle.
6. Install the MSI chipset package for the X870E board, then the current NVIDIA driver branch that supports the RTX 5090 and WSL CUDA. Reboot. Confirm Device Manager shows the 5090 and Task Manager shows 64 GB RAM.
7. Power plan: High performance. Hardware-accelerated GPU scheduling: on. A fixed page file of 16 GB on the NVMe sits beside the system-managed file so Docker and a Python OOM leave a trace instead of a hard reset.
8. Install, in this order: Windows Terminal, Git for Windows, 7-Zip, Python 3.13 from python.org (for small host-side scripts), VS Code or the editor already used for this repo, and the Kaggle CLI (`pip install kaggle` in that Windows Python, then place `kaggle.json`).

`C:\Users\<user>\.wslconfig`:

```ini
[wsl2]
memory=48GB
processors=24
swap=32GB
networkingMode=mirrored
dnsTunneling=true
localhostForwarding=true
```

The 9950X3D has 16 cores and 32 threads. Leaving 8 threads and about 16 GB of RAM to Windows keeps the desktop usable while vLLM and Docker run. Apply with `wsl --shutdown` after the file is saved.

### 5.2 PowerSpec Ubuntu and the scorer stack

1. `wsl --install -d Ubuntu-24.04`. Create the Linux user. `sudo apt update` and install `build-essential`, `git`, `curl`, `ca-certificates`, `python3`, `python3-venv`, `python3-pip`, `pigz`, and `jq`.
2. Inside WSL, `nvidia-smi` must show the 5090. If it does not, the Windows driver is too old for WSL CUDA. Stop and update the driver before installing frameworks.
3. Install Docker Engine inside Ubuntu using Docker's apt repository for Ubuntu 24.04. Add the user to the `docker` group. Enable the service (`sudo systemctl enable --now docker` after systemd is enabled in `/etc/wsl.conf`).
4. In `/etc/wsl.conf`:

```ini
[boot]
systemd=true

[user]
default=<linux-user>
```

5. Accept the competition rules in the browser on the submitting account. Download the competition dataset if `D:\gemma4\data` is not already a copy of the local package, and download the wheelhouse dataset named in the getting-started notebook (`gemma-4-developer-agent-wheelhouse`). The 22 GB competition zip does not contain those wheels. Copy both onto the NVMe. Verify `tasks.jsonl` has 129 lines.
6. Download `gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct` from the Kaggle model slug used by the competition, revision matching the getting-started path (`google/gemma-4/.../gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct/2`) or the Hugging Face repo `google/gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct` if that revision is the same checkpoint. Record the commit hash in `LOCK.md`.
7. Build the sandbox image from `data/docker`:

```bash
cd /mnt/d/gemma4/docker
docker build -f Dockerfile.sandbox -t swebench-sandbox:latest .
docker run --rm --network none swebench-sandbox:latest python3 --version
docker run --rm --network none swebench-sandbox:latest pytest --version
```

The image is `python:3.13-slim` plus git, patch, pigz, pytest, and the `imp.py` / `telnetlib.py` shims. Confirm Python prints 3.13.x.

8. Create a venv inside WSL and install the wheelhouse with the same filters as the getting-started notebook: skip cutlass `.pth` hacks that do not apply, rewrite `cu128` filenames when the wheel name requires a `+cu128` local version, and `pip install --no-deps` the resulting set. Import `vllm`, `swegemma`, and `adk_submission`. If a wheel fails because the driver CUDA is older than cu128, update the Windows driver rather than mixing a newer vLLM from PyPI. The parser flags have to match the grader.
9. Serve the model on one GPU:

```bash
python -m vllm.entrypoints.openai.api_server \
  --model /mnt/d/gemma4/models/gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct \
  --dtype bfloat16 \
  --max-model-len 32768 \
  --gpu-memory-utilization 0.90 \
  --enable-auto-tool-choice \
  --tool-call-parser gemma4 \
  --reasoning-parser gemma4 \
  --enable-lora \
  --max-loras 8 \
  --max-lora-rank 128 \
  --tensor-parallel-size 1 \
  --port 8000
```

Watch `nvidia-smi`. Record peak memory at startup and after one 4k-token completion. If the process OOMs at 32,768, retry once with KV-cache dtype fp8 and record the flag. A server that only stays up at 16,384 tokens is a lab defect: mark every later score `non-comparable` until 32,768 fits.

10. Run `swegemma eval` on a single `instance_id` (`fastapi_15588` is a small, readable SSE change) with `--sandbox docker`, the sample submission copied to `repo/submission`, and the sample's 1-minute budget replaced locally by `max_time_minutes: 6` and `max_tool_calls: 24`. Success is a `results/<id>/` trace and a Phase-2 log, whether or not the task resolves. Commit `LOCK.md` with driver version, image id, model hash, and the VRAM numbers.

### 5.3 ProArt laptop (same day, after the PowerSpec is installing wheels)

1. Windows Update to idle. Install the NVIDIA driver for the RTX 4060 laptop GPU (the discrete GPU is wired through Advanced Optimus; leave the vendor power profile on a plugged-in "standard" mode so the CPU does not throttle during trace review).
2. Install Git, Windows Terminal, VS Code, and Python 3.13. Install Ubuntu 24.04 via WSL2. Home edition supports WSL2.
3. `.wslconfig` memory is 20 GB, processors 8, swap 8 GB. The machine has 32 GB soldered.
4. Clone `D:\gemma4\repo` over the LAN once the PowerSpec repo exists. Copy `tasks.jsonl`, `graphs\`, and `embeddings\` (about 1 GB together). Leave the 21 GB of snapshots on the PowerSpec and the ROG HDD.
5. This laptop never launches the 31B server. Optional later work is a tiny Gemma E2B sketch for prompt wording. Those runs are labeled `sketch` and are excluded from `GATES.md`.

### 5.4 ROG desktop (same day, overlapping step 8 above)

1. Confirm virtualization is enabled. The i7-12700F has no integrated graphics, so the RTX 3080 is the display adapter. Install the current NVIDIA driver and then leave the GPU idle. Do not run a GPU burn-in. The chassis has a 550 W supply; a 3080 plus a 12700F at full load is the wrong way to spend the power budget. Grading is CPU and RAM.
2. Windows Update, Git, Python 3.13, WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04, Docker Engine inside WSL. `.wslconfig`: memory 48 GB, processors 16, swap 16 GB.
3. Move the Docker data root and the snapshot archive to the 2 TB HDD. Keep the sandbox image and a 20-task hot subset on the 512 GB SSD if space remains after Windows. The compressed snapshots are about 21 GB; extracted work trees are larger, and the SSD will fill if they land there by default.
4. Build `swebench-sandbox:latest` from the same Dockerfile. Run `docker run --rm --network none swebench-sandbox:latest python3 --version` and confirm 3.13.
5. Join the same LAN as the PowerSpec (5 GbE on the PowerSpec; use a wired port on the ROG if one is free). Share `results\` so the ROG can grade patch files the PowerSpec has already written. A simple path is `scp` or an SMB share of `D:\gemma4\repo\results`. NFS is optional and not required on day 0.
6. Smoke test Phase 2 without the model: apply one gold `patch` plus its `test_patch` inside a container and confirm pytest exits 0. This is the grading-farm equivalent of the gold-replay rung.

### 5.5 Day-0 exit

| Check | Machine | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 64 GB RAM, EXPO decision recorded | PowerSpec | `LOCK.md` has the BIOS version and the memory-test result |
| 5090 visible in WSL | PowerSpec | `nvidia-smi` screenshot or paste in `LOCK.md` |
| Context 32768 resident | PowerSpec | Peak VRAM recorded, server answers one tool-call completion |
| Sandbox image | PowerSpec and ROG | `python3 --version` is 3.13 inside `--network none` |
| One agent task | PowerSpec | Trace, patch file, and Phase-2 log exist |
| Gold replay of one task | ROG | Pytest exit 0 for that task's official patch |
| Authoring checkout | ProArt | Repo cloned, `tasks.jsonl` readable |
| Rules | Browser | Accepted on the submitting account |

Stop at midnight even if the model download is still running. A partial `LOCK.md` that says which step failed is the handoff to Thursday morning.

## 6. Sprint 1 — clock and ceiling (2026-10-01 to 2026-10-07)

Theme: measure the ceiling and the grader before improving the prompt. The sample brain is the control. The first upload is a measurement, and its public score is aimed at the day-0 leader (0.17) because the sample's one-minute budget is the obvious defect.

### Thursday 2026-10-01 — splits and gold replay

- PowerSpec: generate the four split files from seed `20260930` (12+8 canary, 18+12 dev, 37+28 pool, 13+1 OOD). Commit them.
- PowerSpec: gold-patch replay on all 20 canary tasks through Docker Phase 2. Target 20/20. Any miss is a harness bug. Fix the lab, not the prompt.
- ROG: the same 20 gold replays, to prove the farm matches the PowerSpec. Differences in exit codes get resolved before Friday.
- ProArt: start `lab/SUBMISSIONS.md` and `lab/GATES.md`.

### Friday 2026-10-02 — three rungs

- PowerSpec, concurrency 1, temperature 0, six-minute cap: file oracle, then symbol oracle, then blind, on the canary. The blind run uses the sample prompt with only the budget changed, so the control is interpretable.
- Queue overnight if needed. One canary pass at about six minutes is about two hours; three rungs are an afternoon plus an evening.
- ProArt: read the blind traces and tally F1–F12 on paper. Do not edit the prompt yet.

### Saturday 2026-10-03 — fork and thinking sweep (M1)

- Write the three counts into `GATES.md`. Apply the fork rule from the roadmap: localization emphasis if file oracle beats blind by 4 or more; literal-repair emphasis if file oracle is at or below 5/20.
- Thinking-budget sweep on 10 dev tasks at 512, 1024, 2048, and 4096, with `max_output_tokens` 4096. Freeze the budget that resolves the most of those 10, breaking ties toward the smaller budget.
- ROG grades any patches the PowerSpec produced, if Phase 2 was deferred to keep the GPU on generation. By Saturday night the farm should be the default grader so the 5090 stays on the agent loop.

### Sunday 2026-10-04 — first real brain, dev only

Build `submission/` v1 from the roadmap spec, still without adapters:

- Root `LlmAgent`, localizer `AgentTool`, four skills, early-edit policy, `eval_config.yaml` at 6 minutes / 24 calls / 90-second commands.
- Drop the sample LoRA directories.
- Run the 30-task dev split. Fix crash bugs (YAML include cycles, skill script exceptions, zip layout). Prompt wording changes wait until Monday so Sunday's run is a clean v1 baseline.

### Monday 2026-10-05 — one dev iteration

- One change, chosen from the most common failure code on Sunday's dev run.
- Re-run dev. Keep the change if it gains two or more dev tasks and does not drop the requests holdout by two or more. The requests run is 13 tasks and can share the GPU overnight.

### Tuesday 2026-10-06 — package and upload (calibration)

- Grep the tree for accidental gold hunks.
- Zip from inside `submission/` so `agent.yaml` is at the archive root. Check unpacked size, extensions, and a single declared model.
- Upload. Record the Kaggle start time.
- While it runs (up to 12 hours), start the setup-time histogram: on the ROG, time snapshot extract plus editable install for all 129 snapshots with no agent. That histogram is the `setup_minutes` term in the clock formula.

### Wednesday 2026-10-07 — read the log and exit sprint 1 (M2)

- From the Kaggle log: number of tasks visited, wall time, and median seconds per model turn.
- Speed ratio = Kaggle seconds per turn ÷ PowerSpec seconds per turn.
- If the ratio is 1.3 or higher, set `max_time_minutes` to `floor(6 / ratio)` with a floor of 3, and set `max_tool_calls` to 16 on the shorter cap so a slow grader still reaches an edit.
- If the log shows the job only contains the public half, raise the cap using the 60-task row of the clock table (a cap of 10 minutes) instead of shrinking it.
- Score gate: canary ≥ 3/20 on the v1 brain, and a public score ≥ 0.17, or a written ceiling-based revision in `GATES.md`.
- Tag `sprint1-exit`.

Sprint-1 contingency: if the upload dies before covering the task list, sprint 2 starts with budgets and startup time, and localization work slips by two days. Coverage is the product.

## 7. Sprint 2 — localization (2026-10-08 to 2026-10-14)

Theme: make graph queries symbol-shaped, and make the first edit early. Emphasis follows the M1 fork. The schedule below assumes the localization fork, which is the expected one given that only 15 of 129 issues name a `.py` path. If the fork was literal-repair, swap this sprint's graph work with sprint 3's literal work and keep the dates.

| Day | PowerSpec | ROG | ProArt |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Thu 10-08 | Implement `harvest_symbols` and `py_grep`. Unit-test them inside the sandbox image on five issues | Time the remaining snapshot setups if Wednesday's histogram is incomplete | Draft the localizer output schema on ten trace excerpts |
| Fri 10-09 | Wire the localizer `AgentTool`. Dev run on 30 tasks | Grade Thursday and Friday patches | Mark F9 (empty English queries) before and after |
| Sat 10-10 | Early-edit rule. Inspect ten traces: first `edit_file` by tool call 4 on issues shorter than 500 characters | Grade | File any trace where the localizer edited the tree. It has no write tools; a write means the YAML is wrong |
| Sun 10-11 | Symbol-oracle versus blind on the dev split, to see whether the graph tools are now worth a call | Grade | Update the failure histogram |
| Mon 10-12 | One repair of the worst remaining F-code. Holdout run on all 13 requests tasks overnight | Grade the holdout | Compare requests traces with fastapi traces for F12 (layout assumptions) |
| Tue 10-13 | Upload if canary, scored this morning, is ≥ 2 tasks above the last upload. Otherwise keep the daily slot | Idle grading queue | Write the public-log notes into `SUBMISSIONS.md` |
| Wed 10-14 | Canary full run. Exit check | Confirm canary Phase-2 matches | Tag `sprint2-exit` |

Exit (M3): F9 under 10% of dev failures, canary ≥ 6/20, requests ≥ 2/13.

The sequential-agent alternate runs on Wednesday only if the root traces still wander after the localizer is in place: a 10-task dev A/B, `SequentialAgent` versus the single root. The winner is the one with more resolves at the same cap. A tie keeps the single root.

## 8. Sprint 3 — repair, literals, hygiene (2026-10-15 to 2026-10-21)

Theme: convert a localized file into a passing hidden test. This is where F5 (wrong literal), F7 (bad `old_string`), F8 (truncated tool call), and F11 (dirty diff) get their fixes. Prompt work freezes on Wednesday.

| Day | Work | Exit that evening |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Thu 10-15 | `patch_hygiene` skill and the `/tmp` probe rule. Re-run the 10 dirtiest dev tasks | Zero F11 in that ten |
| Fri 10-16 | Literal card: localizer must echo quoted strings from the issue into `literals`. Editor prompt says to reuse them unchanged | Five dev tasks that previously failed F5 re-scored |
| Sat 10-17 | Repair turn: one extra `edit_file` only when pytest names the edited file. Wider failures stop the session | Trace audit of 10 tasks shows at most one repair |
| Sun 10-18 | `max_output_tokens` 2048 versus 4096 on 10 long-edit dev tasks. Keep the setting with fewer F8 events | Setting written into `sampling.yaml` |
| Mon 10-19 | Full dev rerun of the frozen candidate. No further wording edits after this run starts | Dev histogram archived |
| Tue 10-20 | Requests holdout plus canary. Upload if the canary gained ≥ 2 since the last zip | Public log archived |
| Wed 10-21 | Reproduce the canary once more at temperature 0. Tag `prompt-freeze` | M4: canary ≥ 8/20, requests ≥ 3/13 |

From Thursday 2026-10-22 onward, prompt and skill changes require a canary gain of two tasks in a single controlled run. Drive-by edits to `coder.md` stop.

## 9. Sprint 4 — adapters (2026-10-22 to 2026-10-28)

Theme: QLoRA on the frozen scaffold. The base model remains the fallback zip.

### Environment

- PowerSpec only. Unsloth or PEFT with the QAT checkpoint, gradient checkpointing, batch size 1, rank 32, alpha 64, dropout 0. Sequence lengths: 4k for the localizer, 8k for the editor.
- If a 8k editor step OOMs, drop to 4k and pack a single hunk plus the surrounding function, not the whole file. Record the truncation rule in `LOCK.md`.
- The ROG keeps grading. The ProArt builds the JSONL manifests and spot-checks 20 rows a day for template fidelity.
- Optimizer state and checkpoints go to `D:\gemma4\train\`. Keep the last two checkpoints only. A rank-32 adapter is a few hundred megabytes; Adam state for a 31B LoRA is the disk and RAM risk. 64 GB host RAM plus the 48 GB WSL cap is enough for paged or 8-bit Adam at batch 1. If the Linux OOM killer fires, switch to 8-bit Adam before shrinking rank.

### Data built Monday–Tuesday style, actually Thursday–Friday

| Day | Data work |
| --- | --- |
| Thu 10-22 | Export resolved pool trajectories from sprints 1–3 into the `gemma4` tool format. Format-lock: 20 hand-checked calls parse under the competition vLLM flags |
| Fri 10-23 | Build localizer cards from gold hunks on the pool only. A card's file set must be a subset of the gold files. Drop the 26-file outlier and any example over 3 files |
| Sat 10-24 | Synthetic bugs: 100 single-function mutations in permissively licensed public repos of similar size, each with a fail-to-pass test the agent will never see at inference. Run the frozen brain. Keep successes |
| Sun 10-25 | Train `localizer` overnight. Monday morning, canary and requests at temperature 0 |
| Mon 10-26 | Train `editor` overnight on pool successes plus Saturday's synthetic successes |
| Tue 10-27 | Evaluate localizer alone, editor alone, and both. Ship an adapter only if it gains ≥ 2 canary tasks and does not drop requests by ≥ 2 |
| Wed 10-28 | Tag `sprint4-exit`. Upload the winning zip if it earned the canary gain. If no adapter qualifies, upload nothing and keep `prompt-freeze` as the leader |

Teacher drafts are allowed on pool tasks the student still misses, using a small general-purpose API subscription. Each draft is rewritten into the tool schema and must pass Phase 2 before it enters the mixture. The teacher is not used on canary, requests, or httpx.

Exit (M5): format-lock 20/20, and either a qualified adapter or a written decision that the prompt-freeze brain remains the submission.

## 10. Sprint 5 — rejection sampling (2026-10-29 to 2026-11-04)

Theme: a second training round on traces the student itself resolved, plus a budget retune if the speed ratio drifted.

| Day | Work |
| --- | --- |
| Thu 10-29 | Generate 4 trajectories at temperature 0.3 for each pool task the current brain misses. Cap each generation at the submission's `max_time_minutes`. Queue on the PowerSpec; grade on the ROG |
| Fri 10-30 | Generation continues. Expect on the order of 65 pool tasks × 4 × 6 minutes, which is about 26 GPU-hours if every task runs to the cap. Concurrency 2 is allowed only if Sunday's VRAM log from day 0 showed 4 GB free at batch 1. Otherwise stay at concurrency 1 and let the queue run into Saturday |
| Sat 10-31 | Filter to Phase-2 passes. Inspect 15 kept traces for format and for test-file edits. Drop the rest |
| Sun 11-01 | Train the second-round localizer and editor from the sprint-4 mixture plus the kept traces |
| Mon 11-02 | Canary and requests, temperature 0, one trajectory. Roll back if requests drops by 2 or more |
| Tue 11-03 | Recompute the speed ratio if a new Kaggle log exists. Adjust `eval_config.yaml` only when the ratio changed by 0.2 or more. Upload when the canary gate is met |
| Wed 11-04 | Tag `sprint5-exit`. Archive the mixture manifest (repo, commit, license, and whether a row is student or teacher) |

Exit (M6): canary ≥ 11/20, requests ≥ 4/13, mixture manifest complete.

A reinforcement-learning probe is optional and small: one GRPO-style or rejection-only comparison on 20 pool tasks, reported in the paper, adopted in the zip only if it beats Monday's adapter on a fresh canary run. The default submission path is the supervised second-round adapter. A full RL run on 31B with minute-long episodes does not fit this week alongside the rejection-sampling queue.

## 11. Sprint 6 — freeze (2026-11-05 to 2026-11-11)

Theme: two candidates, each reproduced, plus a paper draft. No new features after Monday.

| Day | Work |
| --- | --- |
| Thu 11-05 | Candidate A is the best public zip so far. Candidate B is the best canary-plus-requests zip, which may be the same commit. Rebuild both from the tagged commits in a clean directory |
| Fri 11-06 | Canary run for A and for B. A mismatch with the recorded score blocks that candidate until the diff is explained |
| Sat 11-07 | Second reproduction of whichever candidate matched on Friday. Requests holdout once for B |
| Sun 11-08 | Paper draft from `GATES.md`, the ceiling table, the failure histogram, and the adapter ablation. Sections follow the host's invited topics: tuning, code comprehension, and graph use |
| Mon 11-09 | Last allowed change: a packaging or crash fix only. Any prompt change resets the reproduction clock and slips that candidate out of the paper day |
| Tue 11-10 | Final zips, checksums, and `SUBMISSIONS.md` entries. Upload the candidate that has not yet been the latest public submission, if its canary is at least tied with the current public leader's backing zip |
| Wed 11-11 | Tag `sprint6-exit`. Paper PDF assembled. M7: both candidates have two matching canary runs on record |

## 12. Final window (2026-11-12 to 2026-12-02)

The six build sprints end on 2026-11-11. The competition does not. This window spends uploads only on reproduction failures and on a single pre-registered improvement if sprint 6 left one hypothesis untested. It does not reopen prompt brainstorming.

| Date | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Thu 2026-11-12 | Submit the paper to the paper-track competition, or write the one-paragraph skip into `GATES.md` before 23:59 UTC. M8 |
| Fri 2026-11-13 to Wed 2026-11-25 | At most two diagnostic uploads, each backed by a canary gain of 2 tasks measured before the upload. Accept the rules reminder and freeze the roster before the entry deadline on the 25th. M9 |
| Thu 2026-11-26 to Mon 2026-11-30 | Quiet period. Re-run candidate A and candidate B canaries once each to catch environment drift. Rebuild zips if the environment changed, and upload the rebuilt zip the same day |
| Tue 2026-12-01 | Select the two finals on the Kaggle submission page. Default selection: the highest public score, and the candidate with the higher requests-holdout count. If those are the same zip, the second final is the prompt-freeze brain, which is the conservative fallback. MF |
| Wed 2026-12-02 | Spare upload slot. Use it only when Tuesday's selection points at a zip that fails the local format-lock or exceeds the size limit. Otherwise do not upload |

Winner obligations, prepared during the quiet period so they are ready if the private leaderboard names this team: training commands, `LOCK.md`, the mixture manifest, and a short method note that a stranger can follow. License on that repository: Apache-2.0.

## 13. Weekly operating rhythm after day 0

| When | What |
| --- | --- |
| Thursday | Start the sprint's largest GPU job. Confirm the ROG grader is consuming `results\` |
| Friday | Read traces on the ProArt. One hypothesized change, written down before the rerun |
| Saturday | Long eval. The operator is not required to sit with it. A failed job pages via the log file, checked twice |
| Sunday | Scoreboard update in `GATES.md`. Decide the Monday change or the Monday freeze |
| Monday | Implement one change |
| Tuesday | Upload day, and only if the canary rule says so. Package checklist every time |
| Wednesday | Sprint exit tag, or a written slip with a new exit date inside the same week |

Package checklist, every upload:

1. One root file, `agent.yaml`, at the zip root.
2. Every agent model string is `gemma-4-31b-it-qat-w4a16-ct`.
3. Adapters, if present, contain `adapter_config.json` and `adapter_model.safetensors`, rank ≤ 128, and the canary note that qualified them.
4. Unpacked size under 3 GiB. Extensions are in the harness allowlist.
5. No `..`, no symlink, no absolute include.
6. Contamination grep against gold hunks is clean.
7. Git tag equals the zip checksum recorded in `SUBMISSIONS.md`.

## 14. What "finished" means

The lab is finished when two submissions are explicitly selected, each of those commits reproduces its recorded canary count, `LOCK.md` can rebuild the environment, and the paper track has either an upload or a written skip. The private leaderboard is then the judge. The day-0 public bar of 0.17 is the first mile, not the finish.

## 15. Day-0 command appendix

These are the commands the Thursday handoff expects to have been run. They assume Ubuntu 24.04 in WSL on the PowerSpec and a data tree at `/mnt/d/gemma4`.

```bash
# Windows, once, after .wslconfig is saved
wsl --shutdown
wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04

# Ubuntu
sudo apt-get update
sudo apt-get install -y build-essential git curl ca-certificates \
  python3 python3-venv python3-pip pigz jq

nvidia-smi
docker build -f /mnt/d/gemma4/docker/Dockerfile.sandbox \
  -t swebench-sandbox:latest /mnt/d/gemma4/docker
docker run --rm --network none swebench-sandbox:latest python3 --version

# Single-task eval once the wheelhouse venv and the model server exist.
# Flags mirror the getting-started notebook and HARNESS_README.md.
swegemma eval \
  --tasks /mnt/d/gemma4/data/tasks.jsonl \
  --snapshots-dir /mnt/d/gemma4/data/snapshots \
  --submission-dir /mnt/d/gemma4/repo/submission \
  --results-dir /mnt/d/gemma4/repo/results/day0 \
  --sandbox docker \
  --task-id fastapi_15588 \
  --max-tool-calls 24 \
  --max-time-minutes 6 \
  --concurrency 1
```

The vLLM launch line in section 5.2 is the one that must succeed before `swegemma eval` is trusted. A green eval on the subprocess sandbox does not satisfy M0.
